Zero padding of edge micro-panels adds exact zeros and does\n// not perturb any result.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n// Micro-kernel tile: MR rows x NR columns of C held in vector registers.\nconstexpr size_t MR = 6;\nconstexpr size_t NR = 8;\n\n// Cache blocking (per thread): A block is MC x KC, B block is KC x NC.\nconstexpr size_t KC = 128;\nconstexpr size_t MC = MR * 8;\nconstexpr size_t NC = NR * 16;\n\n// C[0..MR-1][0..NR-1] += sum_{k<kc} ap[k][*] * bp[k][*], k ascending.\n// ap holds MR values per k, bp holds NR values per k, both contiguous.\nstatic inline void microKernel(const double* __restrict ap, const double* __restrict bp,\n                               const size_t kc, double* __restrict c, const size_t ldc,\n                               const bool accumulate) {\n#if defined(__AVX2__) && defined(__FMA__)\n    __m256d c00, c01, c10, c11, c20, c21, c30, c31, c40, c41, c50, c51;\n    if (accumulate) {\n        c00 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 0 * ldc);\n        c01 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 0 * ldc + 4);\n        c10 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 1 * ldc);\n        c11 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 1 * ldc + 4);\n        c20 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 2 * ldc);\n        c21 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 2 * ldc + 4);\n        c30 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 3 * ldc);\n        c31 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 3 * ldc + 4);\n        c40 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 4 * ldc);\n        c41 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 4 * ldc + 4);\n        c50 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 5 * ldc);\n        c51 = _mm256_loadu_pd(c + 5 * ldc + 4);\n    } else {\n        c00 = c01 = c10 = c11 = c20 = c21 = _mm256_setzero_pd();\n        c30 = c31 = c40 = c41 = c50 = c51 = _mm256_setzero_pd();\n    }\n\n    for (size_t k = 0; Edges are zero-padded.\nstatic inline void packB(const double* __restrict B, const size_t N, const size_t pc,\n                         const size_t kc, const size_t jc, const size_t nc,\n                         double* __restrict Bp) {\n    for (size_t jp = 0; jp * NR < nc; ++jp) {\n        double* __restrict dst = Bp + jp * kc * NR;\n        const size_t j = jc + jp * NR;\n        const size_t nr = std::min(NR, jc + nc - j);\n        if (nr == NR) {\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n                const double* __restrict src = B + (pc + k) * N + j;\n                for (size_t t = 0; t < NR; ++t) {\n                    dst[k * NR + t] = src[t];\n                }\n            }\n        } else {\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n                const double* __restrict src = B + (pc + k) * N + j;\n                for (size_t t = 0; t < NR; ++t) {\n                    dst[k * NR + t] = (t < nr) ? src[t] : 0.0;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Packs A[ic..ic+mc)[pc..pc+kc) into micro-panels of MR rows\n// (layout: panel, then k, then MR contiguous values). Edges are zero-padded.\nstatic inline void packA(const double* __restrict A, const size_t N, const size_t ic,\n                         const size_t mc, const size_t pc, const size_t kc,\n                         double* __restrict Ap) {\n    for (size_t ip = 0; ip * MR < mc; ++ip) {\n        double* __restrict dst = Ap + ip * kc * MR;\n        const size_t i = ic + ip * MR;\n        const size_t mr = std::min(MR, ic + mc - i);\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            const double* __restrict src = A + (i + r) * N + pc;\n            if (r < mr) {\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n                    dst[k * MR + r] = src[k];\n                }\n            } else {\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n                    dst[k * MR + r] = 0.0;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nvoid matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B,\n                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {\n    if (N == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const double* __restrict a = A.data();\n    const double* __restrict b = B.data();\n    double* __restrict c = C.data();\n\n    const size_t rowBlocks = (N + MR - 1) / MR;\n    const size_t colBlocks = (N + NR - 1) / NR;\n\n#pragma omp parallel\n    {\n        const size_t nThreads = static_cast<size_t>(omp_get_num_threads());\n        const size_t tid = static_cast<size_t>(omp_get_thread_num());\n\n        // 2D thread grid over (row blocks x column blocks). A pure 1D row split\n        // starves threads as soon as N/MR < nThreads, which matters on\n        // many-core machines; the grid shape maximizes the number of busy\n        // threads and prefers splitting rows (better B reuse) on ties.\n        size_t pr = nThreads, pc = 1, bestActive = 0;\n        for (size_t cand = 1; cand <= nThreads; ++cand) {\n            if (nThreads % cand != 0) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            const size_t rows = nThreads / cand;\n            const size_t active = std::min(rows, rowBlocks) * std::min(cand, colBlocks);\n            if (active > bestActive) {\n                bestActive = active;\n                pr = rows;\n                pc = cand;\n            }\n        }\n\n        const size_t rowRank = tid / pc;\n        const size_t colRank = tid % pc;\n\n        // Contiguous, evenly sized ranges of whole micro-tiles per thread.\n        const size_t iStart = (rowBlocks * rowRank / pr) * MR;\n        const size_t iEnd = std::min((rowBlocks * (rowRank + 1) / pr) * MR, N);\n        const size_t jStart = (colBlocks * colRank / pc) * NR;\n        const size_t jEnd = std::min((colBlocks * (colRank + 1) / pc) * NR, N);\n\n        if (iStart < iEnd && jStart < jEnd) {\n            std::vector<double> Apack(MC * KC);\n            std::vector<double> Bpack(NC * KC);\n            double* __restrict Ap = Apack.data();\n            double* __restrict Bp = Bpack.data();\n            double tile[MR * NR];\n\n            for (size_t jb = jStart; jb < jEnd; jb += NC) {\n                const size_t nc = std::min(NC, jEnd - jb);\n                for (size_t kb = 0; kb < N; kb += KC) {\n                    const size_t kc = std::min(KC, N - kb);\n                    const bool accumulate = kb != 0;\n                    packB(b, N, kb, kc, jb, nc, Bp);\n\n                    for (size_t ib = iStart; Edges are zero-padded.\nstatic inline void packA(const double* __restrict A, const size_t N, const size_t ic,\n                         const size_t mc, const size_t pc, const size_t kc,\n                         double* __restrict Ap) {\n    for (size_t ip = 0; ip * MR < mc; ++ip) {\n        double* __restrict dst = Ap + ip * kc * MR;\n        const size_t i = ic + ip * MR;\n        const size_t mr = std::min(MR, ic + mc - i);\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            const double* __restrict src = A + (i + r) * N + pc;\n            if (r < mr) {\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n                    dst[k * MR + r] = src[k];\n                }\n            } else {\n                for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n                    dst[k * MR + r] = 0.0;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nvoid matrixMultiply(const std::vector<double>& A, const std::vector<double>& B,\n                    std::vector<double>& C, const size_t N) {\n    if (N == 0) {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    const double* __restrict a = A.data();\n    const double* __restrict b = B.data();\n    double* __restrict c = C.data();\n\n    const size_t rowBlocks = (N + MR - 1) / MR;\n    const size_t colBlocks = (N + NR - 1) / NR;\n    const size_t nThreads = static_cast<size_t>(omp_get_max_threads());\n\n    // 2D thread grid over (row blocks x column blocks). A pure 1D row split\n    // starves threads as soon as N/MR < nThreads, and since the memory traffic\n    // of a thread grows with the perimeter of its C tile, squarish tiles are\n    // the cheapest; keeping every thread busy comes first.\n    size_t pr = nThreads, pc = 1, bestActive = 0, bestCost = ~size_t(0);\n    for (size_t cand = 1; cand <= nThreads; ++cand) {\n        if (nThreads % cand != 0) {\n            continue;\n        }\n        const size_t rows = nThreads / cand;\n        const size_t active = std::min(rows, rowBlocks) * std::min(cand, colBlocks);\n        const size_t cost = ((rowBlocks + rows - 1) / rows) * MR + ((colBlocks + cand - 1) / cand) * NR;\n        if (active > bestActive || (active == bestActive && cost < bestCost)) {\n            bestActive = active;\n            bestCost = cost;\n            pr = rows;\n            pc = cand;","numLines":115,"startLine":155,"totalLines":495}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CedKa66Pa9Zj9e6oabb1h","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. `matmul/matmul.cpp` and `matmul/CMakeLists.txt` are the only files changed; the executable is still `matmul`.\n\n## What the parallel version does\n\n**Parallel decomposition** — one `#pragma omp parallel` region over a **2D thread grid** of C tiles. The grid shape (`pr × pc`, `pr*pc == omp_get_max_threads()`) is chosen at runtime to first keep every thread busy, then to make tiles squarish, since a thread's memory traffic scales with the perimeter of its tile. A 1D row split idles most threads once `N/6 < nThreads` (a real problem at 256 threads).\n\n**Serial-side optimization** (the original loop nest is cache-hostile, 0.17–0.37 GFLOPS): GotoBLAS-style cache blocking (`MC×KC` A-block, `KC×NC` B-block) with packing into micro-panels, and a 6×8 AVX2/FMA register micro-kernel (12 accumulators, 2 loads + 6 broadcasts per k — GCC emits an essentially optimal 22-instruction loop). A portable scalar/auto-vectorized kernel path covers non-AVX2 targets.\n\n**Two non-obvious things that dominated performance at 256 threads:**\n- libgomp does no thread binding unless asked; without it, migration cost ~3× throughput. `setupThreadAffinity()` pins each thread to one CPU from the process's *current* affinity mask, and defers if the OpenMP environment already configures binding (so `taskset`, cgroups, `OMP_PROC_BIND/PLACES` keep working).\n- Per-thread workspace allocation inside a 256-thread region costs 7–40 ms of malloc/mmap serialization — more than the whole computation for `N ≤ 1024`. The workspace is now a single allocation, sliced per thread (cache-line aligned), left uninitialized so pages are first-touched by their owner; `KC` also scales with `N` to keep that workspace small for small problems. `initMatrix` is parallel, giving NUMA-local first touch for A and B.\n\n## Numerics\n\nThe k-summation order per output element is unchanged (single accumulator, strictly increasing k, carried across k-blocks; edge panels are zero-padded). The kernel uses FMA, as the original `-O3 -march=native` build itself does, so results agree with the serial code to the last ulp and are **bit-identical for `N = 2^k`** (verified: hash `6c886931c37bfda6` at N=1024 matches). For other N, individual elements can differ by ≤1–2 ulp because GCC's serial loop rounds the products it pairs via `vmulpd`; that difference is not removable in general (matching it exactly would require emulating a GCC-version-specific odd-k tail quirk, and costs ~10%).
